#### R3-121 · Low · The run's `held` map is updated after two awaits, so two seats of one payer in the same wave both pass `decide` with the full balance

- **Where:** `src/lib/briefing/runner.ts:202` · **Journey:** existing member · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `held` exists so teammates briefed in the same run do not each assume the whole balance (decide.ts:25 'teammates briefed first'). But it is read at 178 and 197 and written only at 202, after `await claimBriefing` (180) and `await loadSheet` (183). With CONCURRENCY 6 (`mapLimit(todo, 6, …)`), two members of one team can both be between 178 and 202, both see `held = 0`, both plan `ai`, and both reserve. The ledger's `credit_reserve` only checks spendable ≥ the ceiling, not the day's own charge, so the 'dayCharge first' rule the decide step is for is not enforced for the second seat.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A team owner with two running seats and a balance a penny or two above two days' picks. Both seats are in the same wave of six; both are briefed and charged (~1p each). At 07:00 the picks pass finds the owner short for the second seat's pick, and that profile's Today's 5 is 'left out and named' — because of two 1p briefings the decide step was written to prevent.
- **Evidence:** runner.ts:178 `if (!adminUser && !((spendable ?? 0) - (held.get(payer.payerId) ?? 0) > 0))` … 180 `await claimBriefing` … 183 `await loadSheet` … 197 `decide({ …, heldThisRun: held.get(payer.payerId) ?? 0, … })` … 202 `held.set(payer.payerId, …)`.
- **Smallest fix:** Reserve in the map before the awaits: at line 178, `held.set(payerId, held + dayCharge + ceilingEstimate)` right after the £0 check (the ceiling is computable before the sheet: the system prompt is fixed and the user message is bounded), then subtract the ceiling again when the plan is not `ai` or the claim fails.

#### R3-122 · Low · The 3p ceiling is enforced on a token estimate only: `chargeOnce` meters the real tokens with no cap, and a reserved debit never checks the balance or the reservation's maximum

- **Where:** `src/lib/briefing/runner.ts:300` · **Journey:** existing member · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `decide` rejects `ceiling > 3` where ceiling = `approxTokens(system + user) + 50` × price + 400 × output price (line 196). After the write, `chargeOnce` meters `written.outputTokens` and `written.inputTokens` as returned by the API with no comparison to the reservation or to BRIEFING_CEILING_PENCE. In the ledger, `credit_debit` with `p_reservation` only does `update credit_reservations set settled_base_pence = settled_base_pence + v_remaining` and skips the spendable check entirely (schema.sql ~783-786), and `meter` passes `allowNegative: true`, so a charge above the hold goes into the overdraft grant. In practice it is bounded: I measured the prompt at ~1,000 estimated tokens (system 2,624 chars + user 660-960 chars), giving a Haiku ceiling of ~1.2p, and an overrun would need the real input to exceed the estimate by five times the unused output allowance. So this is a missing guard, not a live overcharge.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A sheet whose JSON tokenises denser than 3.5 chars/token and a reply that uses most of its 400 output tokens: the debit is a fraction of a penny above the 1.2p reservation, lands with no check, and nothing in code would stop a larger overrun if the prompt grew (new angles 4/5/6/10 are planned).
- **Evidence:** runner.ts:300-301 `await meter({ provider: 'anthropic', unit: units.output, quantity: written.outputTokens, skipPreflight: true, … })` / `quantity: written.inputTokens`; schema.sql credit_debit: `if p_reservation is not null then update credit_reservations set settled_base_pence = settled_base_pence + v_remaining where id = p_reservation and user_id = p_user; else select spendable_base_pence …`; meter.ts:171 `allowNegative: true`.
- **Smallest fix:** In `chargeOnce`, compute `wouldCharge` (already done at 231) and, if it exceeds `ceiling`, meter scaled quantities so the total equals the ceiling (or add a `maxBasePence` to `meter` as the chat's `settle()` does), and assert `charge <= BRIEFING_CEILING_PENCE` with a logged warning.

#### R3-142 · Low · BRIEFING_CEILING_PENCE (3p) is a code constant, not a billing_settings row, unlike every other Batch 23b-26 price and the README's 'never more than 3p'

- **Where:** `src/lib/briefing/decide.ts:17` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `export const BRIEFING_CEILING_PENCE = 3;` is the one briefing price gate and is hard-coded, while the kill switch next to it (`briefings_enabled`) and every Batch 25/26 number are `billing_settings` rows edited in admin. The brief's rule is 'prices read from billing_settings'. Zac cannot raise or lower the ceiling (for the Sonnet trial, say, whose ceiling I measured at ~2.4p and would pass, or to tighten Haiku) without a deploy.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Zac decides after the trial that Sonnet is worth it and wants a 4p ceiling: the only way is a code change; /admin/billing shows the switch but no ceiling.
- **Evidence:** decide.ts:16-17 `/** The most a briefing may be charged, in base pence (Zac's default: 3p). */ export const BRIEFING_CEILING_PENCE = 3;` and decide.ts:41 `if (i.ceiling > BRIEFING_CEILING_PENCE) return { kind: 'template', reason: 'over_ceiling' };`
- **Smallest fix:** Seed `briefing_ceiling_pence` = 3 in the Batch 23b schema section, read it in `getBillingSettings` beside `briefings_enabled`, and pass it into `decide` as `ceilingMax`.

#### R3-143 · Low · A drafted entry's variants are the members' phrasings cut at 300 characters while the entry limit is 200, so a long question produces a draft Zac cannot approve until he trims it by hand

- **Where:** `src/lib/knowledge/gap/run.ts:395` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** redactQuestion slices to GAP_QUESTION_MAX_CHARS (300) and si_conversation_questions.question may be up to 500 chars; the draft's variants take the first five samples as they are. checkContent refuses a variant over VARIANT_MAX_CHARS (200) with 'A phrasing is over 200 characters.' The draft note records it as 'Fix before approving', so the Sonnet spend is kept but the result is an unapprovable draft.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member types a 250-character question in the full view; the nightly job drafts an answer and the entry's draft_note says 'Fix before approving: A phrasing is over 200 characters.' Zac has to edit the variant before Approve works.
- **Evidence:** run.ts:395 `variants: samples.map(redactQuestion).filter((s) => s && s.toLowerCase() !== draft.question.toLowerCase()).slice(0, 5),` · prompts.ts:30 `.slice(0, GAP_QUESTION_MAX_CHARS)` (300) · render.ts:167 `if (v.length > VARIANT_MAX_CHARS) errors.push(...)` (200)
- **Smallest fix:** `variants: samples.map(redactQuestion).filter(...).map((s) => s.slice(0, VARIANT_MAX_CHARS)).slice(0, 5)` (import VARIANT_MAX_CHARS from ../config), or drop samples longer than the limit.

#### R3-144 · Low · A model call that times out (and is retried once by the SDK) counts 0p towards the monthly cap although Anthropic may have billed one or two completed requests

- **Where:** `src/lib/knowledge/gap/models-server.ts:65` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** The client is built with `timeout: GAP_MODEL_TIMEOUT_MS, maxRetries: 1`, so on a timeout the SDK sends the request again; if either attempt completed server-side its usage is never seen, and the catch returns `costPence: 0`, so recordCost adds nothing and no provider_calls row is logged. The cap (fitsCap on si_gap_runs.cost_pence) is therefore an under-count on a slow night.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A Sonnet draft call hangs past 60 s, the SDK retries and that also times out; both ran to completion at Anthropic (≈4p each), the run records 0p and goes on drafting up to the cap as if nothing was spent.
- **Evidence:** models-server.ts:50 `const client = new Anthropic({ apiKey, timeout: GAP_MODEL_TIMEOUT_MS, maxRetries: 1 });` · models-server.ts:65 `return { text: null, usage: empty, costPence: 0, stopReason: null, error: timeout ? 'timeout' : ... };` · run.ts:382 `await recordCost(reply.costPence);`
- **Smallest fix:** Set `maxRetries: 0` (the job already retries the next night) and on a timeout add the call's worst case to report.costPence via recordCost(worst) so the cap counts it.

#### R3-148 · Low · If a Haiku/Sonnet unit_costs row is missing the job prices every call at 0p, so the monthly cap can never trip and the run spends freely

- **Where:** `src/lib/knowledge/gap/plan.ts:34` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `price(unit)` falls back to 0 when the row is absent (run.ts:279), worstCasePence and usagePence are then 0, fitsCap(spent, 0, cap) passes for any cap > 0, and meter only logs a warning. callModel refuses only when MODEL_UNITS lacks the model, not when the price table lacks the unit. Live has the eight rows today, so this is a guard against a deleted or renamed row.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Someone deletes the sonnet55_output_token row on /admin/billing; that night's run records 0p for every Sonnet draft and the £15 cap is never reached.
- **Evidence:** run.ts:279 `const price = (unit: string) => table.get(unitKey('anthropic', unit))?.unitCostPence ?? 0;` · plan.ts:51 `return capPence > 0 && spentPence + worstPence <= capPence;` · meter.ts:111 `console.warn(`[credit] no unit cost for ${charge.provider}:${charge.unit} — charging 0 until a row exists`);`
- **Smallest fix:** In runGapJob, after building `price`, check all eight units of MODEL_UNITS resolve to > 0 and otherwise stop with `report.stopped = 'no_price'` (or throw before any model call).

#### R3-10 · High · Five of the seed's own questions can never be answered by the chat: after stop words and synonyms they tie with another entry's phrasing, so the matcher returns low_confidence and the member gets "I don't know that one yet" (logged as a gap)

- **Where:** `src/lib/knowledge/match.ts:176` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** matchIndex requires the top entry to lead the second by MATCH_MIN_LEAD (0.05, config.ts:175). tokens() drops STOPWORDS ('why', 'did', 'you', 'me', 'question', 'it', 'what', 'does', …) and folds SYNONYMS (phone→call, charged→price, much→price), so an approved entry's exact question collapses to one token that another entry's variant also collapses to, and both score 1.0. The chat (src/lib/chat/knowledge-server.ts:51) treats anything but 'answered' as no approved answer, says "I don't know that one yet" and records a could-not-answer/low_confidence question for the nightly job. The approved text is right there; the matcher refuses to pick it.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Ran matchKnowledge over the 44 seed entries at the live thresholds (0.55 / 0.3), channel 'chat': "Why did you call me?" (why_called's own question) → calls:1 why_called:1 → low_confidence; "What does it cost?" (what_it_costs' own question) → chat_cost:1 what_it_costs:1 → low_confidence; "How much credit do I have?" (credits' own variant) → chat_cost:1 credits:1 → low_confidence; "How do I turn on auto top-up?" (auto_topup's own variant) → auto_topup:1 topup:0.967 → low_confidence; "How do I top up?" → topup:0.746 auto_topup:0.707 → low_confidence. On channel 'call' "Why did you call me?" and "What does it cost?" tie the same way. A new member's first typed question, "What does it cost?", is answered "I don't know that one yet" although Zac approved exactly that question.
- **Evidence:** match.ts:176 `const outcome: MatchOutcome = top >= o.answerMin && top - second >= MATCH_MIN_LEAD ? 'answered' : top >= o.lowMin ? 'low_confidence' : 'could_not_answer';` · config.ts:175 `export const MATCH_MIN_LEAD = 0.05;` · config.ts:138–139 STOPWORDS include 'why', 'question', 'did', 'you', 'me' · config.ts:148 `phone: 'call'`, config.ts:145 `charged: 'price'` · knowledge-server.ts:51 `if (match.outcome !== 'answered') return { outcome: match.outcome, answers: [], topConfidence };` · scratch run output: `low_confidence "Why did you call me?" calls:1 why_called:1 standout_calls:0.807` and `low_confidence "What does it cost?" chat_cost:1 what_it_costs:1 calls:0.673`
- **Smallest fix:** In matchIndex, before the lead rule, short-circuit a verbatim hit: normalise the question (lower-case, strip punctuation and spaces) and compare with each entry's question and variants the same way; if it equals a phrasing of the top-scoring entry and of no other entry, return 'answered' regardless of the lead. Then take 'question' and 'why' out of STOPWORDS so "Why did you call me?" and "Why was I charged for a question?" keep a distinguishing token. Add the five questions above to match.test.ts.
- **Verification:** code path: stands (High) — CONFIRMED by reading the code path and reproducing the run locally (read-only scratch script over SEED at the live thresholds 0.55/0.3). The code does what the finding says. match.ts:176: `const outcome: MatchOutcome = top >= o.answerMin && top - second >= MATCH_MIN_LEAD ? 'answered' : top >= o.lowMin ? 'low_confidence · reproduce: stands (High) — REPRODUCED the core claim, and it is wider than stated; but two of the five examples and the 'call' channel claim do not hold at the entry points, and no money is lost. 1. The matcher mechanics are exactly as cited. match.ts:176 `const outcome: MatchOutcome = top >= o.answerMin && top - second >= MATCH_MIN_LEAD ? 'answ · rule and severity: stands (High) — The mechanics stand, confirmed from the code and a reproduction; the scope is narrower than claimed. 'low_confidence' : 'could_not_answer';` with config.ts:175 `

#### R3-21 · Medium · PRODUCT_FACTS.reportSections = 10 is the report's maximum, not its count: a standard Full analysis renders 7–8 sections, so the analyser_output answer ("A 10-section report") is wrong on calls and in the chat for most members

But the report's sections are chosen per report: src/lib/pdf/sections.ts:75-79 sectionsFor() filters ALL (verdict, numbers, market, second_opinion, location, setup, deal, project, diligence, plan) by what the report has, and StayfulReport.tsx:59-71 renders second_opinion, setup, deal, project, diligence and plan only when present. All 10 appear only for a project deal with a PMI second opinion. A member's own Full analysis without PMI on a non-project deal has at most 8 (verdict, numbers, market, location, setup, deal, diligence, plan), and an address they typed themselves (no deal) 7. Approval's figure check cannot catch this: the figure is a placeholder, and placeholders resolve from code, so the answer passes checkContent and goes out as approved on the phone and in the chat with a number the member's own PDF contradicts (its running header says e.g. `03 / 08`).

Boolean(has.diligence) : s.id === "project" ? Boolean(has.project) : s.id === "second_opinion" ? Boolean(has.secondOpinion) : s.id === "plan" ? has.plan !== false : true,` · StayfulReport.tsx:62/68/69/70: `{data.secondOpinion ? <PageSecondOpinion …> : null}` … `{data.project ? <PageProject …> : null}`
- **Smallest fix:** Stop quoting a count: reword analyser_output without {report_sections} (list what every report has), and either delete reportSections from PRODUCT_FACTS or make the placeholder a range resolved from sections.ts (count of the always-present sections to ALL.length) so the figure cannot be wrong for any report. Re-approve the entry.

#### R3-32 · Medium · The chat's "Want me to remember that?" stores the member's whole typed question in si_member_facts.asked, unchecked for sensitive topics and never shown or deletable on its own

- **Where:** `src/lib/chat/full-server.ts:317` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** C1
- **What is wrong:** When the full view proposes a fact, the proposal is saved as `{ fact: factProposal, asked: question }` — `asked` is the member's own typed question (up to 500 chars), not the question the page asks them. On Yes, log-server.ts:276 passes `asked: proposal.asked` into rememberFact, and facts-rules.ts:43 only trims and slices `asked` to 300 chars: `sensitiveTopic` and the PERSONAL regexes (email, link, phone/card number, full postcode) run on `fact` alone (lines 40-42). si_fact_remember then stores it in si_member_facts.asked (schema.sql:7205). rememberedFactsFor selects only `id, fact, channel, confirmed_at` (facts-server.ts:107) and RemembersSection renders `f.fact`, so the member never sees what was stored beside the fact, and the gap job's redaction never touches this table. On calls the same column holds the agent's own question ("Want me to remember you prefer 2-beds?"), which is what the rules assume.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member types "I'm going through a divorce and I'm after a 2-bed in LS6 I can live off". The model calls propose_fact("Wants a 2-bed in LS6"); the page asks "Want me to remember that? “Wants a 2-bed in LS6”"; they tap Yes. The fact passes every check, and si_member_facts.asked now holds the full sentence including "divorce" (a SENSITIVE_TERMS.money word that would have refused it as a fact). Account shows only "Wants a 2-bed in LS6". The same happens with a phone number or full postcode typed in the question.
- **Evidence:** full-server.ts:317 `factProposal: factProposal ? { fact: factProposal, asked: question } : null` · log-server.ts:276 `rememberFact({ ..., fact: proposal.fact, asked: proposal.asked, confirmed: true, channel: 'chat' ...})` · facts-rules.ts:43 `const asked = typeof input.asked === 'string' && input.asked.trim() ? input.asked.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim().slice(0, FACT_ASKED_MAX_CHARS) : null;` (no topic/personal check) · facts-server.ts:107 `.select('id, fact, channel, confirmed_at')`
- **Smallest fix:** In full-server.ts:317 store the page's real question, e.g. `asked: 'Want me to remember that?'` (or null), not the member's text; or make checkFact run sensitiveTopic and PERSONAL on `asked` as well and drop it (null) when it fails.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — CONFIRMED from the code on main (42e68ea); every quoted line is verbatim. Chain: src/lib/chat/full-server.ts:317 `factProposal: factProposal ? { fact: factProposal, asked: question } : null` — `question` is the member's typed text (MAX_QUESTION_CHARS = 500, src/lib/chat/config.ts:41). It is persisted into chat_turns.fa

#### R3-35 · Medium · A price or percentage written in words bypasses the figure guard ("5 pounds", "8 pence", "10 percent")

- **Where:** `src/lib/chat/guard.ts:200` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** C2
- **What is wrong:** FIGURE only treats £, p, % and k/m/bn as units; isExempt() lets any plain whole number 0–10 through as a count. So a money or percentage figure the model writes without a symbol is a 'count'. Run against the code with only '£4.00' and '£12.40' allowed: checkFigures('A full analysis is 5 pounds.') → ok, '8 pence' → ok, 'About 10 percent occupancy.' → ok, while '£5' is refused.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Quick box: approved answer says £4; the model writes "A full analysis is about 5 pounds"; shown and charged.
- **Evidence:** export function isExempt(f: Figure): boolean {
  return !f.money && !f.percent && Number.isInteger(f.value) && f.value >= 0 && f.value <= 10;
}
- **Smallest fix:** Extend FIGURE's suffix group with \s?(pounds?|pence|quid|per\s?cent|percent) (money / percent kinds) so a worded unit is checked like a symbol.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — CONFIRMED from the code and by running the guard. The finding's line number is wrong (guard.ts is 139 lines; the mechanism is lines 29 and 66–67), but the mechanism is exactly as described. src/lib/chat/guard.ts:29 — the only units FIGURE recognises are symbols stuck to the digits: const FIGURE = /(£)?\s?(\d{1,3}(?:,\d

send({ type: 'delta', text: event.delta.text });

#### R3-43 · Medium · SENSITIVE_TERMS.people lists bare pronouns (he, she, him, her, his, hers), so a fact the chat tool asks for "in the third person" ("He only wants 2-bed flats") is refused as sensitive after the member taps Yes

- **Where:** `src/lib/knowledge/config.ts:128` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** config.ts:128 puts the bare pronouns `'he', 'she', 'him', 'her', 'his', 'hers'` in SENSITIVE_TERMS.people, and facts-rules.ts:27-33 sensitiveTopic() matches them as whole words on every fact. But the chat's propose_fact schema (src/lib/chat/tools.ts:129) tells the model `'The fact, short, in the third person.'`, and the call agent is told to pass 'the fact in a few words' (src/lib/voice/agent/prompt.ts:34) with no form given. A fact written as the tool asks ("She prefers Leeds", "Only wants flats near her work") fails checkFact with reason 'sensitive' detail 'people' (verified with a scratch node --test: "He only wants 2-bed flats" -> {ok:false, reason:'sensitive', detail:'people'}; "Only wants 2-bed flats" -> ok). The page has already shown the fact and asked "Want me to remember that?"; on Yes, answerFact (src/lib/chat/log-server.ts) stamps the proposal answered and calls rememberFact, which refuses, and /api/chat/facts answers 'I can’t keep that kind of detail, so I haven’t saved it.' — about a plain property preference. The proposal is one-shot (fact_proposal.answered is claimed first), so it cannot be retried.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member types "I only want 2-bed flats" in the full view. Sonnet, following the schema's "in the third person", calls propose_fact with "He only wants 2-bed flats". The answer shows "Want me to remember that?" with that text; the member taps Yes and reads "I can’t keep that kind of detail, so I haven’t saved it." Nothing is saved, Account shows nothing, and the member has just been told their deal-type preference is too sensitive to keep.
- **Evidence:** config.ts:128: `people: ['wife', 'husband', 'partner', … 'neighbor', 'he', 'she', 'him', 'her', 'his', 'hers'],` · src/lib/chat/tools.ts:129: `fact: { type: 'string', description: 'The fact, short, in the third person.' }` · facts-rules.ts:40-41: `const topic = sensitiveTopic(fact); if (topic) return { ok: false, reason: 'sensitive', detail: topic };` · src/app/api/chat/facts/route.ts:19: `sensitive: 'I can’t keep that kind of detail, so I haven’t saved it.'` · scratch test output: `"He only wants 2-bed flats" -> people {"ok":false,"reason":"sensitive","detail":"people"}`
- **Smallest fix:** Drop the six bare pronouns from SENSITIVE_TERMS.people (keep the relationship nouns), or change the propose_fact description to the subject-less form its own example uses ("Only wants 2-beds") and add a test that a pronoun-led property preference passes checkFact.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — Confirmed from the code and reproduced. config.ts:128 lists bare 'he','she','him','her','his','hers' under SENSITIVE_TERMS.people (present since the original Batch 24 commit 7047a6d); facts-rules.ts:27-33 matches every term as a whole word on every fact and checkFact:40-41 refuses with reason 'sensitive'. #### R3-93 · Low · A refused Yes to "Want me to remember that?" is stamped answered, so the member is told to make room but can never say Yes again

- **Where:** `src/lib/chat/log-server.ts:273` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** answerFact claims the proposal before rememberFact runs (line 273: `update({ fact_proposal: { ...proposal, answered: yes ? 'yes' : 'no' } }) ... .is('fact_proposal->>answered', null)`), then on a refusal writes `{ ...proposal, answered: 'yes', saved: result.ok }` (line 277) and returns the reason. The next tap hits line 271 `if (!proposal || proposal.answered) return null`, which /api/chat/facts (route.ts:43) turns into 'That has already been answered.' The page agrees: RememberThat (ChatAnswer.tsx:106) replaces the Yes/No buttons with the message on any reply, and on a network error sets 'That didn't save. Please try again.' with no button left to try. So the 'cap' message — 'Delete something in Account to make room.' — and the network-error 'Please try again' both point at an action that is no longer possible for that fact.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member at the 30-fact cap is offered 'Want me to remember that? "You prefer flats"', taps Yes, reads 'I'm remembering as much as I can for you. Delete something in Account to make room.', deletes two facts in Account, comes back to the answer: the buttons are gone, and a re-POST of {turnId, yes:true} answers 'That has already been answered.' The fact is lost unless they re-ask the question (another charged full-view answer).
- **Evidence:** log-server.ts:271 `if (!proposal || proposal.answered) return null;` · :277 `await admin.from('chat_turns').update({ fact_proposal: { ...proposal, answered: 'yes', saved: result.ok } }).eq('id', turnId);` · facts/route.ts:22 `cap: 'I’m remembering as much as I can for you. Delete something in Account to make room.'` · :43 `if (!result) return Response.json({ ok: false, message: 'That has already been answered.' });` · ChatAnswer.tsx:106 `if (message) return <p ...>{message}</p>;`
- **Smallest fix:** In answerFact, on `!result.ok` with a retryable reason ('cap', and the transient failure path) write back `{ ...proposal }` without `answered` so the claim is released; in RememberThat keep the buttons visible when the reply is `ok:false` with reason 'cap' or on a network error (only hide them on ok:true, 'duplicate', 'sensitive', 'personal_details').

#### R3-19 · Medium · A member who pays within 48 hours of joining is never judged on the deals that went live before they paid: the paid window has passed and the free window is not tested for paid members

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/run.ts:305` · **Journey:** sign-up · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** Which window a deal must fall in is decided by the member's tier NOW, not the tier they had when the deal went live. Line 305: `if (retrying || (m.paid ? inPaid : inFree || (own && inPaid))) list.push(row);`. A paid member is only tested against the paid window (`paidFrom` = the last pass's `paidThrough` minus 15 min, line 168); a free member only against the free window (`freeTo = now − freeDealDelayHours`, line 167). A deal that went live while the member was free (so it was not judged: not yet in the free window) and whose free window opens after they have paid (so it is not judged: no longer in the paid window) is never judged for them, and no decision row is written, so nothing retries it. `hasEverPaid` (server.ts:129) is what `m.paid` means, so this is exactly the ad member's path: sign up free, buy a pack within two days.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Sam signs up from the ad on Monday 09:00 (never paid). A 2-bed in Harrogate goes live at 10:00. The 10:35 pass puts it in the paid window only; Sam is free, so `inFree` is false and it is skipped with no row. Sam buys a pack Monday evening. On Wednesday 10:35 the deal enters the free window, but Sam is now `paid`, so only `inPaid` is tested, which is false. It is never judged, never saved, never called about. Every deal that went live in Sam's first 48 hours is lost the same way; a member who stays free gets them all.
- **Evidence:** run.ts:305 `if (retrying || (m.paid ? inPaid : inFree || (own && inPaid))) list.push(row);` · run.ts:167–168 `const freeTo = new Date(now.getTime() - delayMs).toISOString(); const paidFrom = new Date((last.paidThrough ? Date.parse(last.paidThrough) : now.getTime() - FIRST_LOOKBACK_MS) - OVERLAP_MS).toISOString();` · server.ts:129 `paid: hasEverPaid(r, { admin: isAdminEmail(r.email ?? null) })`
- **Smallest fix:** Line 305: test a paid member against both windows, `m.paid ? inPaid || inFree : inFree || (own && inPaid)`. The `existing` map (line 291–294) already stops a deal judged in the paid window from being judged again in the free one, so paid members who were paid all along cost nothing extra.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — CONFIRMED from the code on main (42e68ea). 1. The tier is decided at pass time, not at live time. src/lib/standout/server.ts:129: `paid: hasEverPaid(r, { admin: isAdminEmail(r.email ?? null) })`, computed fresh from `profiles` on every pass. src/lib/access.ts:211-219: `hasEverPaid` returns true once `last_topup_at` (or

#### R3-20 · Medium · A transient profile read failure is written down as the member's choice: the standout's call is replaced by 'Saved for you' for good and admin reads 'calls are off or no consent'

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/notify.ts:86` · **Journey:** reveal · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** planPass (notify-server.ts:119) reads `memberFacts(userId)`, which returns null when the profiles read errors. planMember then calls `cannotCall(switchedOn, null)`, which returns 'calls_off' (notify.ts:86), so the step is `{ action: 'email', status: 'calls_off' }` and applyStep writes `notify: 'email', call_status: 'calls_off'` on the decision (notify-server.ts:183). notify is claimed once (`.is('notify', null)`), so the decision is never replanned: the member's one standout call about this deal never happens, the deal goes in "Saved for you" instead, and /admin/standout shows 'No call: calls are off or no consent' (reasons.ts:62) for a member whose calls are on. The same conflation is in startCall: `ENQUEUE_STATUS = { … unknown_member: 'calls_off' }` (notify-server.ts:210) maps enqueueCall's `skipped unknown_member` (memberFacts null again) to a permanent 'email'. By contrast the other two reads in the same loop (`placed === null || queued === 'error'`, line 123) are treated as 'wait' and retried next pass, and calls-server.ts's own rule is "every read that fails answers wait" (lines 216, 222, 227).
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** The 14:35 standout pass saves a 95% match for a member with calls on and a verified number. Supabase times out on the profiles read inside memberFacts (one of ~8 reads per member in the loop). The decision is stamped notify 'email', call_status 'calls_off'; the call is never queued; the next morning's email carries the deal under "Saved for you"; /admin/standout says the member has calls off. Zac checks their Account → Notifications: calls are on.
- **Evidence:** notify.ts:84-87 `export function cannotCall(switchedOn: boolean, m: NotifyMember | null): string | null {\n  if (!switchedOn) return 'deal_calls_off';\n  if (!m) return 'calls_off';` · notify-server.ts:119 `const m = await memberFacts(userId);` · notify-server.ts:122-124 `placed === null || queued === 'error' ? list.map((r): NotifyStep => ({ decisionId: r.id, action: 'wait', status: 'pending' }))` · notify-server.ts:210 `const ENQUEUE_STATUS: Record<string, string> = { calls_off: 'calls_off', not_owner: 'not_owner', management_no_deals: 'management_only', no_number: 'no_number', disabled: 'deal_calls_off', unknown_member: 'calls_off' };` · member-server.ts:46-49 `if (error || !p) { … return null; }`
- **Smallest fix:** In planPass, treat `m === null` like the other failed reads: `placed === null || queued === 'error' || m === null ? wait steps : planMember(...)`. In startCall, map `r.outcome === 'skipped' && r.reason === 'unknown_member'` to `again('error')` instead of ENQUEUE_STATUS. Then `cannotCall(…, null)` is unreachable from the server and the test at notify.test.ts:44 can assert 'wait' instead.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — Confirmed from the code on main (42e68ea), every link in the chain quoted: 1. memberFacts returns null on a profiles read error (src/lib/voice/member-server.ts:46-49): `if (error || !p) { if (error) console.error('[voice] member read failed (schema behind?):', error.message); return null; }`. The other reads inside it

#### R3-49 · Medium · In BST the nudge, the below-floor text/email and deal calls take the day's email slot before the 08:10 UTC digest, so a digest-only member's Today's 5 is dropped, not moved

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/nudge-server.ts:212` · **R2:** R2-46 · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** JE
- **What is wrong:** Three Batch 25 senders call claimCallSlot (SendKind 'si_call', slot 'daily') after sending: sendNudge (nudge-server.ts:212), belowFloor (notify-server.ts:307) and a placed deal call (queue-server.ts:252). The text window is 08:00–20:00 UK and outbound hours 09:00 UK, so in BST the standout cron's 07:35 UTC pass sends nudges and below-floor texts, and si-calls places deal calls from 08:00 UTC — all before daily-digest at 08:10 UTC. digest-run reads slotsInUse and records 'slot_used' for that member with no retry, so a member who had no 07:00 picks email (no charged pick, changes-only, free teasers) gets no daily email at all that day. R2-46 reported this for intro/low-credit calls; Batch 25 adds three senders that fire on exactly these mornings (a member low on credit is the one most likely to be digest-only).
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Tuesday in BST: a pay-as-you-go member at £4 has a nudge claimed overnight; 07:35 UTC the nudge text+email go (42p) and take the daily slot; 08:10 UTC the digest skips them (slot_used). Their Today's 5 for that day is never sent.
- **Evidence:** nudge-server.ts:212 `await claimCallSlot(r.user_id, now);`; notify-server.ts:307 `if (texted || emailed) await claimCallSlot(m.userId, now);`; queue-server.ts:252 `await claimCallSlot(m.userId, now);`; uk-time.ts:13-15 WINDOW_START_HOUR = 8 (UK); digest-run.ts:185 `else if (slots?.has(p.id)) perUser.push({ user: p.id, sent: false, reason: 'slot_used' });`; vercel.json standout `35 * * * *`, daily-digest 08:10 UTC.
- **Smallest fix:** In claimCallSlot (cap-server.ts) skip the claim when `now` is before LOW_CREDIT_ALONE_FROM_MINUTE_UTC (08:30 UTC), the same guard lowCreditMayGoAlone uses, so an early text or call never consumes the slot the morning emails need; or have processNudges/belowFloor wait for the first pass after 08:30 UTC.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — Confirmed from main (42e68ea). vercel.json:211 runs the standout pass hourly at :35 and :103 the daily digest at 08:10 UTC; run.ts:211,217 run processNotifications and processNudges in every :35 pass with no hour gate. nudge-server.ts:147 gates only on inSendingWindow (uk-time.ts:13, 08:00 UK), so in BST the 07:35 UTC 

#### R3-54 · Medium · "Saved for you" is only read for members with daily picks on, while the standout saves for every account: a picks-off or inactivity-paused member is never told about a standout that was saved instead of called

- **Where:** `src/lib/notify/digest-run.ts:292` · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** SWEEP1
- **What is wrong:** The standout audience is every profile (src/lib/standout/server.ts:105-108 `loadMembers` reads all `profiles`; rules.ts:110-113 `memberSkip` skips only team_member / no_primary_profile / client_profile / profile_paused). When the member cannot be rung (calls off, no verified number, below the floor, a third standout in the month) notify-server sets `notify: 'email'` and relies on "the next daily email's Saved for you" (README §23). But the only two readers of those rows gate on the picks audience: digest-run reads `savedForYouFor(admin, open.filter(wantsTeasers).map(...))` and passes `savedForYou: wantsTeasers(p) ? ... : null`, where `wantsTeasers` = sourcing_alerts true, welcome checked, not paused, not `inactivePausedIds`; picks-run's audience is `.eq('sourcing_alerts', true)` (line 310) and skips `inactive` (line 424). So for a member with Daily picks off, or paused for 25 days' inactivity (Batch 20), the `standout_decisions` row stays `notify='email', emailed_at null` for ever: no call, no text, no email, nothing on Today. The deal sits in My deals under the label without the member ever having been told.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member turns Daily picks off in Account → Notifications and has calls off. The 10:35 standout pass saves a 95% match to their My deals with notify='email' (notify-server.ts:253). The 08:10 digest the next morning builds them a changes-only email if they have alerts waiting, or nothing: `savedForYou` is null because `wantsTeasers(p)` is false. /admin/standout shows the save as 'email' with no emailed date, indefinitely. The same happens for a member whose picks were paused for inactivity, who is exactly the member the standout was meant to bring back.
- **Evidence:** digest-run.ts:191 `const wantsTeasers = (p: ProfileRow) => p.sourcing_alerts === true && p.welcome_checked_at !== null && !isPaused(p) && !inactive.has(p.id);` · :292 `const savedForYou = await savedForYouFor(admin, open.filter(wantsTeasers).map((p) => p.id), base);` · :349 `savedForYou: wantsTeasers(p) ? savedForYou.get(p.id) ?? null : null,` · picks-run.ts:310 `.eq("sourcing_alerts", true)` · standout/rules.ts:110-113 only `team_member`, `no_primary_profile`, `client_profile`, `profile_paused` · email-server.ts:37 `.eq('notify', 'email')`
- **Smallest fix:** Either add a `picks_off` (and `inactive`) reason to `memberSkip` fed from `profiles.sourcing_alerts` and `inactivePausedIds` in standout/run.ts, so the standout never saves for someone no email can reach; or in digest-run read `savedForYouFor` for every `open` member and pass `savedForYou` without the `wantsTeasers` gate (buildDaily already makes a 'todays_5' email from saved lines alone; the unsubscribe label would then need 'Stop these emails' for a picks-off member).
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — CONFIRMED from code. Standout judges every profile: src/lib/standout/server.ts:108 reads `profiles` with no sourcing_alerts filter, run.ts:272/332 and rules.ts:110-117 (memberSkip) never return a picks-off or inactive reason; `grep sourcing_alerts|inactivePausedIds|inactiv src/lib/standout` is empty. notify.ts:99/104/1

#### R3-65 · Medium · 'Saved for you' only ever reaches members with daily picks on: a picks-off or paused member who cannot be called is saved a deal and told by no channel, and the decision waits for an email that never comes

- **Where:** `src/lib/notify/digest-run.ts:349` · **Journey:** existing member · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** The two senders of "Saved for you" both gate it on picks: the digest reads `savedForYouFor(admin, open.filter(wantsTeasers)…)` (line 292) and passes `savedForYou: wantsTeasers(p) ? … : null` (line 349); the picks run reads it for `dailyIds` only (picks-run.ts:1239, members getting a pick). `wantsTeasers` is sourcing_alerts true, welcome checked, subscription not paused, not inactivity-paused. The standout pass judges every account regardless (server.ts loadMembers; memberSkip has no picks reason), and notify-server's plan sends such a member to 'email' whenever they cannot be rung (calls off, no number, team, monthly limit, a second standout, deal calls off: notify.ts:104-117). The decision then sits at `notify 'email', emailed_at null` indefinitely (email-server.ts:37-39 keeps selecting it, nothing ever sends it), and the member learns of the save only if they happen to open My deals. The README (§23) promises "members who can't be called hear in the next daily email's 'Saved for you'" — for these members there is no daily email.

Tuesday, Wednesday… the same. /admin/standout shows the save with notify 'email' and no emailed_at for weeks; the member never hears. - **Smallest fix:** Either skip the standout for members with picks off or paused — add a memberSkip reason 'picks_off' in run.ts fed by `sourcing_alerts === false || picks_paused_inactive_at || isPaused(p)` — so nothing is saved that cannot be told; or, if saves should still happen, let the digest carry "Saved for you" without the wantsTeasers gate: include members with pending `standout_decisions(notify='email', emailed_at null)` in `byId`/`open` the way alert-waiting members are (digest-run.ts:127-152) and drop the `wantsTeasers(p) ?` on line 349 (buildDaily already makes a 'todays_5' email from saved items alone, message.ts:487, 539).
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — Confirmed from the code. Both senders of "Saved for you" gate on daily picks: src/lib/notify/digest-run.ts:292 `savedForYouFor(admin, open.filter(wantsTeasers)...)` and :349 `savedForYou: wantsTeasers(p) ? ... : null` (wantsTeasers at :191 = sourcing_alerts true && welcome checked && !isPaused && !inactivity-paused); s

#### R3-71 · Medium · Switching STANDOUT_CALLS_ENABLED off, even briefly, blocks every queued deal call for good and uses up each deal's one call; SI_CALLS_ENABLED off merely leaves them queued

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/calls-server.ts:212` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** dealCallStillWanted runs inside placeCall just before dialling (queue-server.ts:178). Its first line returns `{ ok: false, reason: 'deal_calls_off' }` when the env switch is off. placeCall treats any `!w.ok` without `wait` as final: `dropOrBlock(call, w.reason)` sets the row to status 'blocked' (queue-server.ts:184-186). The deal's one call is then consumed (si_calls_log_deal_uidx is on any status), syncPass maps the block to `blocked:deal_calls_off, notify null` (notify.ts:166) and the next plan's enqueueCall hits the index → 'exists' → `notify 'email', call_status 'blocked:deal_calls_off'` (notify-server.ts:252-253). Nothing restores the call when the switch goes back on. Batch 23's own switch behaves differently: run.ts:107 (`if (!out.enabled && o.apply) continue`) leaves calls queued while SI_CALLS_ENABLED is off. The calls-server header lists this check under "the last check before a queued deal call is dialled" with reads that "answer don't call" — but the other soft failures in the same function return `wait(...)` and are retried an hour later (lines 216, 222, 227, 239).
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Zac sets STANDOUT_CALLS_ENABLED=false on a Wednesday evening because a deal-link text read oddly, meaning to turn it back on next morning. Thursday 09:00 the si-calls cron finds 6 deal calls queued since Wednesday afternoon, each is blocked 'deal_calls_off' and their decisions move to "Saved for you". He re-enables at 09:30: no call is made about any of the six; /admin/standout reads 'Blocked: deal calls off' for members who were due the month's first call.
- **Evidence:** calls-server.ts:212 `if (!dealCallsEnabled()) return { ok: false, reason: 'deal_calls_off' };` · queue-server.ts:184-186 `if (!w.ok) {\n      if (o.apply) await dropOrBlock(call, w.reason);\n      return { outcome: 'blocked', reason: w.reason };` · run.ts:107 `if (!out.enabled && o.apply) {` (SI_CALLS_ENABLED off: calls left queued) · schema.sql:7476 `create unique index if not exists si_calls_log_deal_uidx on public.si_calls_log (user_id, trigger_ref) where call_type = 'deal';`
- **Smallest fix:** In dealCallStillWanted return `wait('deal calls off')` instead of a reason, so placeCall defers the call an hour (the existing DEAL_RETRY_MS path); a switch left off for four days then ends as 'stale' as today. Or, smaller still, in run.ts skip placing call_type 'deal' rows while `!dealCallsEnabled()`, the same way it skips everything while `!out.enabled`.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — Confirmed from main (42e68ea). src/lib/standout/calls-server.ts:212 `if (!dealCallsEnabled()) return { ok: false, reason: 'deal_calls_off' };` is a hard reason while the other soft checks in the same function (lines 216, 222, 227, 239) return `wait(...)`. src/lib/voice/queue-server.ts:179-187 defers only when `'wait' i

- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member verifies a mobile for calls only, misses the intro, and gets (and pays 22p for) a text they never switched on.

- **Smallest fix:** Use sendMemberText for every fallback text, or check contactCanReceive before sendSms.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Low) — CONFIRMED in a narrowed form; the finding's example and claimed mechanism are wrong and must be rewritten before it is reported. What is true (quoted): - src/lib/voice/fallback-server.ts:53-56 — `type === 'deal' ? await sendMemberText(admin, {...}) : await sendSms({ to: m.phone, ... })`. Only the deal fallback honours 

#### R3-79 · Low · R2-84 partly fixed: only deal texts honour the Texts switch; contact-card and auto-topup texts still ignore sms_contacts.enabled

- **Where:** `src/lib/voice/tools-server.ts:163` · **R2:** R2-84 · **Journey:** intro call · **Readers:** D
- **What is wrong:** send_template_text routes deal_link through sendMemberText (contactCanReceive → enabled checked) but contact_card and auto_topup_link still go through sendSms with memberFacts.numberOk, which reads verified_at and stopped_at only (member-server.ts:57). The same split is in fallback-server.ts:53-56.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member with 'Texts: Off' in Account but calls on gets, and pays 22p for, the contact card during the intro and the missed-call text afterwards.
- **Evidence:** tools-server.ts:163-165 `which === 'deal_link' ? await sendMemberText(…) : await sendSms({ to: m.phone, … })`; member-server.ts:57 `numberOk: Boolean(contact?.verified_at && !contact.stopped_at && isUkMobile(phone))`
- **Smallest fix:** Route every call text through sendMemberText (kind 'call' added to sms_messages.kind) or add `contact.enabled` to numberOk for texts.

#### R3-81 · Low · R2-48 partly fixed: the intro and low-credit call texts (missed-call fallback, contact card, auto top-up link) still go through sendSms with no sms_messages row, so Twilio's delivery status, the admin text log and sms_monthly_cap never see them

- **Where:** `src/lib/voice/fallback-server.ts:56` · **R2:** R2-48 · **Journey:** intro call · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** Batch 25 routed only deal texts through sendMemberText (which inserts an sms_messages row with a statusCallback and re-checks the Texts switch). The intro/low_credit fallback text (fallback-server.ts:56) and the in-call contact_card / auto_topup_link texts (tools-server.ts:165) still call sendSms directly with no messageId and no statusCallback: /api/twilio/status has no row to update, the Batch 8 admin SMS log and the 8-a-month cap do not count them, and a Twilio 21610 refusal does not call stopNumber (sendMemberText does).
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member's missed-intro text bounces (Twilio status 'undelivered'); the status callback can find no row, so the text shows nowhere, and the contact card the agent sends on the callback the next day is the member's 3rd uncounted text that month.
- **Evidence:** fallback-server.ts:53-56 `type === 'deal' ? await sendMemberText(admin, {...}) : await sendSms({ to: m.phone, body: missedCallText(type, ctx), purpose: 'si_missed_call', dryRun: callsDryRun() });` and tools-server.ts:162-165 `which === 'deal_link' ? await sendMemberText(...) : await sendSms({ to: m.phone, body, purpose: `si_${which}`, dryRun: callsDryRun() });` — sendSms (src/lib/sms/send.ts:51-61) takes messageId/statusCallback only when given.
- **Smallest fix:** Send those three texts through sendMemberText with a new sms_messages.kind 'call' (widen sms_messages_kind_check in place), which also closes R2-84's remaining half.

#### R3-82 · Low · R2-52 still open: the missed-call text and email go after the member switched calls off between the dial and the webhook

- **Where:** `src/lib/voice/fallback-server.ts:41` · **R2:** R2-52 · **Journey:** intro call · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** sendMissedCallFallback reads memberFacts (which carries callsOn) but never checks it; the only gates are numberOk/phone for the text and email for the email. A member who turned 'Calls from Stayful Intelligence' off while the phone was ringing (or in the minutes before the webhook) is still texted and emailed 'I tried to call you', charged 22p + 20p, and the intro email says 'I'll only call when there's something worth your time'.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** The intro rings at 09:02; the member declines it and switches calls off in Account at 09:03; the post-call webhook at 09:04 sends the text and the email anyway.

- **Smallest fix:** After `const m = await memberFacts(call.user_id)`, return early when `!m.callsOn` (the call was consented to; the follow-up is not).

#### R3-103 · Low · Standout and nudge texts bypass the one-text-a-day slot and sms_monthly_cap

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/texts-server.ts:39` · **R2:** R2-48 · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** D
- **What is wrong:** sendMemberText records the text in sms_messages (kind standout|nudge) but never claims the 'sms' channel slot in notification_sends and is never counted by textsThisMonth (store.ts:323-340 counts notification_sends channel 'sms' only). So a member can get an alert text, a below-floor standout text and a nudge text the same day, and none of them count towards the 8-a-month cap the Text alerts section promises. R2-48 reported the same for Batch 23's call texts; Batch 25 added two more kinds without closing it.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Monday 08:35: the nudge text (22p). 09:10: a deal call's deal_link text (22p). 10:15: sms-alerts sends the day's price-drop text — slotUsedToday is false because none of the earlier texts claimed the sms slot. Three texts in one morning; the alert cap still reads 0 for the month.
- **Evidence:** texts-server.ts:35-46 insertMessage → sendSms → updateMessage, no claimSlot; store.ts:327-331 `.from('notification_sends').select('user_id').eq('channel', 'sms')`
- **Smallest fix:** In sendMemberText claim the sms daily slot (claimSlot(admin, userId, 'deal_changes', now, 'sms')) and include sms_messages kinds standout|nudge in textsThisMonth, or state in README that SI texts are outside the alert cap.

#### R3-104 · Low · A deal call that failed at the provider counts as one of the month's two and uses the deal's one call

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/calls-server.ts:141` · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** D, H25
- **What is wrong:** dealCallsThisMonth counts si_calls_log rows with placed_at set — placeCall stamps placed_at at the queued→ringing claim, before placeOutboundCall, and a refused/timed-out ElevenLabs request leaves the row 'failed' with placed_at kept. si_calls_log_deal_uidx (any status) then stops a second call about that deal, and syncFromCall('failed') → notify null → re-plan → enqueueCall → 'exists' → notify 'email'. A member whose two deal calls both failed at ElevenLabs gets no deal call that month and never a call about those deals.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** ElevenLabs answers 503 on Tuesday's deal call (status failed, never rang) and again on Thursday's. A third standout on Friday: planMember sees placedThisMonth = 2 → 'monthly_limit' → Saved for you only, although the member never heard a ring.
- **Evidence:** calls-server.ts:141 `.eq('call_type', 'deal').not('placed_at', 'is', null).gte('uk_day', …)`; queue-server.ts:219 claim sets `placed_at: now.toISOString()` then :247 `status: 'failed'`; schema.sql:7476 `si_calls_log_deal_uidx … where call_type = 'deal'`
- **Smallest fix:** Count only rows whose status is in ('ringing','answered','missed','voicemail') in dealCallsThisMonth (as nudge-server's PLACED does), and let a 'failed' deal row be retried once by deleting it in syncPass or widening si_calls_log_deal_uidx to `where call_type='deal' and status <> 'failed'`.

#### R3-105 · Low · The standout pass has no overall time budget after the 60 s recheck budget: notify and nudge loops can run past maxDuration 120

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/run.ts:74` · **R2:** R2-35 · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** D
- **What is wrong:** RECHECK_BUDGET_MS stops rechecks at 60 s, but processNotifications (up to 300 decisions, each memberFacts + enqueueCall + an immediate placeCall with ElevenLabs' 15 s timeout) and processNudges (up to 200 sends) have no clock. A pass killed by Vercel after a claimNotify or after sendMemberText but before settleText/finish leaves a guard row claimed (R2-15 shape) and, because recordRun runs last, the watermark never moves, so the whole window is re-judged every hour until a pass finishes.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A busy morning: 40 standouts and 30 nudges due at 08:35; the pass is cut at 120 s mid-sendNudge; the nudge went but credit_nudges stays 'due' and the next pass finds the charge guard taken, sends nothing and marks it skipped:nothing_sent.
- **Evidence:** run.ts:74 `const RECHECK_BUDGET_MS = 60_000;` is the only budget; notify-server.ts:51 `MAX_PER_PASS = 300`; nudge-server.ts:140 `.limit(200)`; run.ts:229-237 recordRun after notifications
- **Smallest fix:** Thread the pass's start time into processNotifications/processNudges and stop starting new members after ~90 s; record the run before the notify step when judging completed.

#### R3-115 · Low · A retargeted deal call keeps the first deal's queued_at, so the 4-day stale rule can drop the better deal's call and use up its one call

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/notify-server.ts:199` · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** retarget moves a waiting call to a better standout by updating only `trigger_ref` (and updated_at) on the si_calls_log row. placeCall measures staleness from `queued_at` (`now - Date.parse(call.queued_at) > STALE_QUEUED_MS`, queue-server.ts:151, 4 days) and blocks the call as 'stale'. The new deal's decision then syncs to `blocked:stale` (notify null), the next plan's enqueueCall hits si_calls_log_deal_uidx (the stale row now carries the new deal's id) → 'exists' → notify 'email'. So a standout saved today can lose its call because the row it inherited was queued four days ago, and 'Saved for you' is all the member gets. Each deferral path in placeCall (hours, next_day, the hourly 'wait' retries at queue-server.ts:180) extends the wait without touching queued_at.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Thursday 18:40: a standout is saved; the call is queued for Friday 09:00. Friday the listing cannot be fetched at 09:00 ('listing could not be checked' → wait, retried hourly) and goes 'unknown' all day; Monday 08:35 a better standout retargets the same row. Monday 19:00 (4 days from Thursday 18:40) placeCall finds it stale and blocks it; the better deal's decision ends 'blocked:stale' → 'Saved for you'. No call about either deal.
- **Evidence:** notify-server.ts:199 `const { data, error } = await admin.from('si_calls_log').update({ trigger_ref: row.deal_id, updated_at: now.toISOString() }).eq('id', step.callId).eq('status', 'queued').select('id');` · queue-server.ts:151-154 `if (now.getTime() - Date.parse(call.queued_at) > STALE_QUEUED_MS) {\n    if (o.apply) await dropOrBlock(call, 'stale');\n    return { outcome: 'blocked', reason: 'stale' };`
- **Smallest fix:** In retarget, reset the clock with the deal: `update({ trigger_ref: row.deal_id, queued_at: now.toISOString(), not_before: <recomputed: now if in hours else nextOpening>, updated_at })`; or in placeCall measure staleness from `max(queued_at, updated_at)` for deal calls.

#### R3-116 · Low · A text the agent sends that Twilio refuses burns one of the call's two text slots for good

- **Where:** `src/lib/voice/tools-server.ts:157` · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** sendTemplateText claims a once-only slot row in si_webhook_events (`<call>:<n>`, provider si_text_slot) before the send; on a refusal it releases the charge guard but never the slot. The per-call limit is then enforced by the slots, not by the ok rows in si_tool_calls (which do not count the failure), so a failed send counts as one of si_texts_per_call_max (2).
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** On a deal call the agent sends deal_link; Twilio refuses it (21211, or the number was just stopped). The agent is told 'didn't go through', sends the contact card (slot 1), and when the member then asks for the top-up link gets 'That's the most texts for one call' after only one text reached the member.
- **Evidence:** tools-server.ts:155-170 `let slot: string | null = null; for (let n = 0; n < textsPerCallMax && !slot; n++) slot = await claimEventSlot(`${call.id}:${n}`); ... if (!r.sent && r.reason !== 'unknown') { await releaseCharge(guard); ... return { ok: false, say: "The text didn't go through..." } }` — no slot release.
- **Smallest fix:** In the `!r.sent && r.reason !== 'unknown'` branch also delete the slot row: `admin.from('si_webhook_events').delete().eq('provider', 'si_text_slot').eq('event_key', slot)`.

#### R3-123 · Low · The 35 s time budget is checked only before a member starts; a member started at 34.9 s can take ~20 s more, so the last wave can run to maxDuration 60 and be killed on the charge path

- **Where:** `src/lib/briefing/runner.ts:159` · **Journey:** existing member · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `if (Date.now() - started > TIME_BUDGET_MS)` is tested once per member, at the top of `briefMember`. After that a member does `loadSheet` (Today-sized reads: `loadTrackedDeals`, `tailoringForMember`, `countDealsAcross`, stage moves), `claimBriefing`, `startAction` (reserve RPC), a writer with a 15 s timeout, `chargeOnce` (actionSpend + two meters, each a provider_calls insert, a debit RPC, a transaction select and an update) and `finishBriefing`. 35 + ~3 + 15 + ~2 + ~1 ≈ 56 s, with six members in that wave; a slow database morning or a 15 s Anthropic stall puts the kill (route maxDuration 60) exactly between the debit and the row update — the precondition for the reclaim findings.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** 06:40 on a morning Anthropic is slow: six last-wave members each wait the full 15 s; the function is cut at 60 s with two of them between `meter` and `finishBriefing`; their rows sit 'generating' with a charge until 06:55.
- **Evidence:** runner.ts:57-58 `/** Inside the route's 60 s, with room to finish the members already started (the writer times out at 15 s). */ const TIME_BUDGET_MS = 35_000;` and 159 `if (Date.now() - started > TIME_BUDGET_MS) { outOfTime.push(m.id); return; }`; route.ts `export const maxDuration = 60;`
- **Smallest fix:** Give the writer `timeout: Math.min(15_000, 55_000 - (Date.now() - started))` and refuse to start a member when less than ~25 s remains (TIME_BUDGET_MS ≈ 25_000), so a kill can only land before any charge.

#### R3-140 · Low · The sourcing cron's ?dry=1 shows neither the 'Saved for you' lines nor the briefing a member's email would carry

- **Where:** `src/lib/listing/picks-run.ts:539` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP1
- **What is wrong:** The dry-run branch returns at line 554 with `wouldEmail` built from slots, plans, alerts and low-credit notices. The Batch 23b briefing read (line 1337) and the Batch 25 `savedForYouFor` read (line 1239) sit after it, so `/api/internal/sourcing?dry=1` cannot show which members would get a 'Saved for you' section or a briefing opener, whereas the digest's dry run reports `savedForYou: built.savedIds.length` (digest-run.ts:394). The brief's check 'every cron's ?dry=1 works' is true for the route but the preview omits the two newest sections of the email.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Zac runs the sourcing dry run before switching STANDOUT_ENABLED on to see who would be told about yesterday's saves. Every `wouldEmail` entry lists `daily.today`, `changes` and `lowCredit` but nothing about saved deals, so he cannot tell from the dry run whether the 07:00 pass or the 08:10 digest will carry them.
- **Evidence:** picks-run.ts:539 `if (dry) { … return done({ status: 200, body: { …, wouldEmail: members.map((m) => { … daily: { slot, tier, today, advice, answerLinks, actFast, changes, changesSwitch, lowCredit, wouldCharge } } }) } }` · :1239 `const savedForYou = opts.ignoreToday ? new Map<string, never>() : await savedForYouFor(admin, dailyIds, siteUrl());` and :1337 `const briefings = await emailBriefingsFor(base, …)` both after the dry return
- **Smallest fix:** In the dry branch read `savedForYouFor(admin, memberIds, siteUrl())` and `emailBriefingsFor(siteUrl(), memberIds)` (both read-only) and add `savedForYou: (savedForYou.get(m.id) ?? []).length` and `briefing: briefings.has(m.id)` to each `wouldEmail` row.

#### R3-149 · Low · An overlapping pass (admin 'Run now' beside the :35 cron) or a retry after a failed status write records a nudge that was sent and charged as 'skipped: nothing_sent'

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/nudge-server.ts:206` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** sendNudge's only guards against a double send are the charge guard rows (`claimCharge('nudge:<id>:text')`, `claimCharge('nudge:<id>:email')`, lines 186 and 197), which correctly stop a second text or email and a second 22p/20p debit. But a second sendNudge for the same still-'due' row then sees both guards taken (`guard` null), leaves `texted` and `emailed` false, and takes the `!texted && !emailed` branch: `finish(admin, r.id, { status: 'skipped', skip_reason: 'nothing_sent' })`. `finish` updates `.eq('status', 'due')`, so whichever pass writes first wins: if the second pass's 'skipped' lands before the first pass's 'sent' (the first is still awaiting settleText/settleEmail/Resend), the row ends 'skipped' and the first pass's `finish('sent')` is a no-op. Two real ways in: (1) /admin/standout → Run now (`runStandoutPassAction` → `runStandout({ apply: true })`, actions.ts:36) runs processNudges with apply while the :35 cron (maxDuration 120) is mid-pass — both read the same 'due' rows (line 140); (2) the first pass's `finish('sent')` fails (logged only, line 159) and the next hourly pass re-sends. The already-reported ':122' finding covers the member side of a skipped row; this is the record: /admin shows the nudge as never sent, text_sent/email_sent false, sent_at null, while the ledger holds the 42p and the member has the text and email.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** Thursday 09:35 UTC the cron is sending 30 due nudges. At 09:36 Zac presses Run now to test a standout. His pass reaches member M's 'due' row while the cron's sendNudge for M is between sendMemberText and settleText: Zac's claimCharge calls return null, his pass writes 'skipped: nothing_sent'; the cron's finish('sent') matches no row. credit_nudges says M was never nudged; M's usage shows 'Text from Stayful Intelligence 22p' and 'Email … 20p'.
- **Evidence:** nudge-server.ts:206-208 `if (!texted && !emailed) {\n    await finish(admin, r.id, { status: 'skipped', skip_reason: dry ? 'dry_run' : 'nothing_sent' });\n    return 'skipped:nothing_sent';` · nudge-server.ts:157-160 `async function finish(admin: Admin, id: string, patch: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<void> {\n  const { error } = await admin.from('credit_nudges').update(patch).eq('id', id).eq('status', 'due');` · charge-server.ts:63-70 claimCharge returns null on 23505 · actions.ts:36 `const r = await runStandout({ apply: true, kind: 'admin' });`
- **Smallest fix:** When `claimCharge` returns null, read the existing guard (`si_call_charges` by charge_key) and count that channel as sent; or in the `!texted && !emailed` branch, before writing 'skipped', check `si_call_charges` for any `nudge:<id>:` key and write 'sent' instead. Cheaper still: claim the row first (`update credit_nudges set status='sending' where id=? and status='due'`), as notify-server's claimNotify does, so a second pass never enters sendNudge.

#### R3-150 · Low · A standout row whose save never ran and which is later re-judged as not standout is never updated: it stays 'standout' with no saved_at, retried hourly for three days and listed on /admin/standout as a standout that was never saved

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/server.ts:309` · **Journey:** admin · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `writeDealDecisions` moves an existing row only when it is 'waiting' (or a forced test), line 309: `return prev !== undefined && !prev.savedAt && (prev.outcome === 'waiting' || (r.forced === true && prev.outcome !== 'standout'));`, and re-attaches a crashed save only when the new outcome is still 'standout', line 328: `if (prev && prev.outcome === 'standout' && !prev.savedAt && r.outcome === 'standout')`. `retryDecisions` (line 185) re-reads every `standout` row with `saved_at` null for three days, so the deal is re-judged each pass; when that re-judgement is `daily_limit`, `not_beating_best`, `not_new` or `gone`, the new row is neither fresh, nor moving, nor a standout, so nothing is written and the row keeps its old outcome for ever.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** The 10:35 pass writes Dan's standout row for the Leeds project and the function is cut off at Vercel's 120 s before `saveStandout` runs (run.ts has no overall budget past the recheck's 60 s). At 11:35 a higher-match Harrogate flat is also standout for Dan; `bestFirst` saves it, taking the day's one save, and the Leeds project is re-judged `daily_limit`. Its row stays `outcome = 'standout', saved_at = null`: no Keep, no label, no call, but /admin/standout shows it as a standout, and every pass for three days loads and re-judges it.
- **Evidence:** server.ts:309 `return prev !== undefined && !prev.savedAt && (prev.outcome === 'waiting' || (r.forced === true && prev.outcome !== 'standout'));` · server.ts:328 `if (prev && prev.outcome === 'standout' && !prev.savedAt && r.outcome === 'standout') ids.set(…)` · server.ts:185 `.or('outcome.eq.waiting,and(outcome.eq.standout,saved_at.is.null)')`
- **Smallest fix:** In `writeDealDecisions`, let `moving` also take `prev.outcome === 'standout' && !prev.savedAt` whatever the new outcome (the update at line 321 already guards with `.is('saved_at', null)`), so the retry's verdict replaces the stale one.

### E. Weekly active tracking

#### R3-42 · Medium · A full-view question that ends in out_of_room, did_not_finish or failed records no si_chat_full activity, so a member whose only action that week was such a question is not weekly active

- **Where:** `src/lib/chat/full-server.ts:336` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `recordActivity(job.user.id, 'si_chat_full', { extras: { outcome } })` sits after the successful settle at the end of the try block. Every exit through `fail()` (lines 123-131: hold ran out `room === 0`, the clock `timeLeft < MIN_ROUND_TIME_MS`, a round cut off while asking for a look-up, a model error, the page going) settles the turn as 'failed' and returns without touching the activity log. The member typed and sent a question either way — the brief's rule E says full-view chat questions count, and README §24 says "A question here counts towards weekly active" without qualification. The quick surface has the same shape (quick-server.ts:192 only on a settled answer) but is record-only, so only the full view affects the one number.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member with a 12p balance asks a deals question in the full view on Tuesday; the hold is too small for a look-up round plus an answer, the model's first round asks for my_deals and the answer ends 'out_of_room' ("I ran out of room on that one before I could answer"). They do nothing else that week. /admin/weekly-active does not count them, although they asked Stayful Intelligence a question; the chat admin's 'weekly active share of chat users' counts them as a chat user (chat_turns row) but not active.
- **Evidence:** full-server.ts:123-131 `const fail = async (state) => { if (!settled) { settled = true; await settle(... { status: 'failed', outcome: null, rounds }, ...) } const reply = stateReply(state, { turnId: turn.id }); if (!live.gone()) send({ type: 'done', reply }); return reply; };` — no recordActivity · :334-336 `// A full-view question counts towards weekly active (Zac, 29 Sep). ... await recordActivity(job.user.id, 'si_chat_full', { extras: { outcome } });` reached only on the settled path · README §24: "A question here counts towards weekly active." · kinds.ts:227 `si_chat_full: inApp(...)` (qualifying).
- **Smallest fix:** Record the question, not the answer: move the `recordActivity('si_chat_full', { extras: { outcome } })` into a finally-style step that runs once per turn whatever the settlement (outcome 'failed'/'out_of_room' in extras), or record it in `prepareFull` after `insertTurn` succeeds (the row is the member's action; a retry with the same clientTurnId returns 'existing' before that point so it is recorded once). Add a test that a fail() path writes one si_chat_full event.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — CONFIRMED from the code on main (42e68ea). In /home/user/STR-Website-2/src/lib/chat/full-server.ts the only `si_chat_full` write is line 336, `await recordActivity(job.user.id, 'si_chat_full', { extras: { outcome } });`, which sits after `settle(...)` (lines 301-322) inside the try block and is reached only when the tu

#### R3-91 · Low · si_chat_full has no dedupeKey, so a second full-view question inside 60 s with the same outcome is dropped by the identical-event rule

- **Where:** `src/lib/chat/full-server.ts:336` · **Journey:** first chat · **Readers:** E
- **What is wrong:** recordActivity is called with `{ extras: { outcome } }` and no dedupeKey. activity_log (schema.sql:2902-2914) then compares the member's latest non-backfilled event: same kind, same (null) deal, identical extras and under 60 s apart → `return null` (not written). The only server-side gap between questions is si_chat_min_seconds (3 s live), so two answered questions a minute apart are one activity row. Weekly active is unaffected (the first row qualifies), but the drill-down's actions and 'actions per visit' undercount every quick follow-up, and the row cannot be tied to its chat_turns row. The same applies to si_chat_quick (quick-server.ts:192), which is record-only so it only affects the record.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A new member asks 'why wasn't I called?' then 'and how many did you check?' 40 s later, both answered: /admin/weekly-active's drill-down shows one 'Asked Stayful Intelligence a question' for two charged questions.
- **Evidence:** await recordActivity(job.user.id, 'si_chat_full', { extras: { outcome } });
- **Smallest fix:** Pass the turn as the key: `recordActivity(job.user.id, 'si_chat_full', { extras: { outcome }, dedupeKey: `si_chat_full:${turn.id}` })` and likewise `si_chat_quick:${turn.id}` in quick-server.ts:192 (a dedupeKey also makes a retry exactly-once by construction).

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/run.ts:364` · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** FI

So the scoring is the same function but the pool is not Today's: a deal Today would never show can be the standout that is auto-saved and rung about. PR #140 says 'It uses the same judgeRow path as Today' and rules.ts says 'no must-have missed (Today's own pool rule)' — the must-haves are Today's, the Not-for-me rules are not.

Today has dropped LS6 and held back project-like listings ever since. On a Tuesday at 10:35 a 3-bed LS6 doer-upper goes live at a 92% match with the profit 30% over their minimum: the standout pass saves it to My deals labelled 'Saved for you', queues a deal call and rings them about the very kind of deal they told Stayful Intelligence not to show.

- **Smallest fix:** In judgeAndSave, for each able member read `const feedback = await feedbackForMember(admin, m.id, now, a.profile.id)` and `const rules = feedbackRules(feedback)` once, skip a row whose `postcode_area` is in `rules.badAreas`, and before `candidates.push` keep only rows that survive `applyCandidateFeedback([{ listing: listingFromRow(row), deal: parseStoredDeal(row.deal), screening: parseScreening(row.screening), project: row.project }], feedback, rules)` (the full-listing pass can stay on the hourly recheck). One test in rules.test.ts or a run test: a 'wrong_area' pass makes the area's deals not_standout with a new reason `not_for_me_rule`.
- **Verification:** code path: stands (High) — CONFIRMED from the code on main (42e68ea). The standout pass judges each deal with judgeRow + judgeStandout and nothing else — src/lib/standout/run.ts:363-365: `for (const row of dealsFor.get(m.id) ?? []) { const judged = judgeRow(row, a.t, wants, now); const j = judgeStandout({ judgement: judged.judgement, facts: judg · reproduce: stands (High) — Walked from the cron entry point. /src/app/api/internal/standout/route.ts:40 calls `runStandout({ apply: !dry, onlyUserId, kind: 'cron' })`; run.ts:184-193 takes every live deal in the paid/free windows (`liveDealsInWindow`), and judgeAndSave (run.ts:270-479) judges each one for each able member with only `judgeRow` +  · rule and severity: stands (Medium) — MECHANICS CONFIRMED. src/lib/standout/run.ts judges each deal with `const judged = judgeRow(row, a.t, wants, now); const j = judgeStandout({ judgement: judged.judgement, facts: judged.facts, figures: judged.figures, wants, chosenTypes: a.types, revealPct: …, settings: s, forced: … })` (run.ts:364-365) and nothing in sr

#### R3-57 · Medium · The nightly grouping closes a gap as 'covered' by an approved entry whatever channel the question came from, so a call question answered only by a view/chat entry is never drafted for calls and the phone agent keeps saying it doesn't know

- **Where:** `src/lib/knowledge/gap/run.ts:301` · **Journey:** later · **Readers:** SWEEP2
- **What is wrong:** `known` (the approved and rejected entries offered to Haiku) carries slug, question and status but no channels, and applyGroup sets the new gap's status to 'covered' whenever the matched entry is approved (run.ts:133). The gap is then excluded from draftCandidates (status must be 'open') and shows on /admin/intelligence/gaps as covered. But the agent only renders entries allowed on 'call' (renderLive('call')), so the caller's question stays unanswerable while the admin page says it is answered. Entries approved for view/chat but not call: credits, pack, save, how_picked, analysis, free_delay, calls, topup, no_match, nightly_rate and the seven chat_* entries.
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** A member rings and asks "Does saving a deal cost anything?"; the agent logs it could_not_answer. That night Haiku matches it to `save` (channels ['view','chat']) → a gap with status 'covered', match_kind 'approved', never drafted. The next caller asking the same gets "I don't know" again, and Coverage counts the question as covered.
- **Evidence:** run.ts:301 `const known = rows.filter((r) => ['approved', 'rejected'].includes(entryStatus(r))).map((r) => ({ slug: r.slug, question: ..., status: entryStatus(r) as 'approved' | 'rejected' }));` · run.ts:133 `const status = entryState === 'approved' ? 'covered' : ...` · prompts.ts:94 `lines.push(`${e.slug} (${e.status}): ...`)` (no channel) · plan.ts:70 `.filter((g) => g.status === 'open' && !g.entry_id && ...)`
- **Smallest fix:** Give the grouping prompt each entry's channels (`${e.slug} (${e.status}; ${e.channels.join('/')})`) and in applyGroup treat a match as 'covered' only when the entry's channels include every channel in the group's questions (`g.questionIds.map(id => qs.get(id)?.channel)`); otherwise leave the gap 'open' with entry_id set so the draft can propose a call variant. Alternatively run grouping per channel.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — Confirmed from the code. run.ts:301 `const known = rows.filter(...).map((r) => ({ slug: r.slug, question: ..., status: entryStatus(r) }))` carries no channels and prompts.ts:94 prints `${e.slug} (${e.status}): ...`, so Haiku cannot tell that an entry is view/chat-only. run.ts:133 `const status = entryState === 'approve

#### R3-60 · Medium · A second standout the same UK day is written off for good as 'daily_limit' instead of being saved without a call

- **Where:** `src/lib/standout/run.ts:445` · **Journey:** existing member · **Readers:** JE
- **What is wrong:** With the live setting standout_max_per_day=1, the second confirmed standout for a member in a day gets a permanent standout_decisions row outcome 'not_standout', reason 'daily_limit'. existingDecisions() then skips that (member, deal) on every later pass ("nothing else is ever judged twice"), and retryDecisions only re-judges 'waiting' rows, so the deal is never saved, never called about and never listed under "Saved for you". Zac's rule was "two standouts in a day → one call" (both saved, one call); the plan already handles that (planMember marks the rest 'one_call' → email).
- **How a member or Zac hits it:** At 10:35 a 3-bed in Harrogate is saved for a member and the call queued; at 14:35 a better-matching 2-bed in York goes live, passes every threshold, and is recorded 'daily_limit'. The member never hears of the York deal on any channel, today or later.
- **Evidence:** run.ts:427 `let left = … savesLeftToday(m.today, s.maxPerDay)`; :445-447 `if (left <= 0) { rows.push(decisionRow(…, 'not_standout', 'daily_limit', …)); continue; }`; :262 `if (prev && !retrying && !(c.o.force && !prev.savedAt)) continue;` with retryDecisions reading only `outcome.eq.waiting` rows (server.ts:197).
- **Smallest fix:** Record the over-the-limit deal as outcome 'waiting' with reason 'daily_limit' so retryDecisions re-judges it on the next UK day (it is then judged against the saved one through savesFor/beatsBest), or set standout_max_per_day to 0 on /admin/standout and let the call plan pick the one call.
- **Verification:** second reader: stands (Medium) — CONFIRMED from the code, with two corrections that keep it at Medium rather than higher. Mechanism (all on main 42e68ea): - src/lib/standout/run.ts:427 `let left = c.o.force ? Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY : savesLeftToday(m.today, s.maxPerDay);` where rules.ts:178-181 returns `Math.max(0, maxPerDay - savedToday)` and serve
